| **[D-1186] [process-gap] `pr-manager-completion-guard` SubagentStop hook defect — no terminal state for a scoped NON-merge pr-manager dispatch** | **OPEN 2026-09-09 — anchored next engine-discipline self-improvement cycle or a dedicated hook-hardening story (E-12, no story ID allocated yet)** | Discovered during PR #824's review-convergence cascade this session: the `pr-manager-completion-guard` SubagentStop hook has no terminal state it recognizes for a scoped NON-merge pr-manager dispatch (e.g. a pr-manager instance dispatched only to run one review cycle, not to complete the full 9-step PR lifecycle) and instead increments its internal "next step" counter uncapped, past the real 9-step lifecycle boundary — this produced an infinite stop-loop this session, requiring the orchestrator to reap the stuck subagent manually. Fix: the hook must recognize an explicit halted/blocked terminal state for scoped dispatches and cap step-increment at the actual lifecycle boundary (9 steps). Not fixed this burst — state-manager does not own hook source (`crates/`); routed to the E-12 Engine Governance follow-up story / next hook-hardening pass. |
| **[D-1186] [process-gap] `precompact-routing.bats` TC-AC004/TC-EC001 flake root-caused to `legacy-bash-adapter.wasm` `exec_subprocess` exit-code handling under CPU contention** | **OPEN 2026-09-09 — anchored next engine-discipline self-improvement cycle or a dedicated exec_subprocess-hardening story (E-12, no story ID allocated yet); human accepted+tracked this session** | Extends the D-1173 single-occurrence `tests/precompact-routing.bats:350` flake note with a root cause discovered this session: the `legacy-bash-adapter.wasm` subprocess (`exec_subprocess`) intermittently returns the wrong exit code under CPU contention — reproduced deterministically under `stress-ng` load at multiple `develop`/`factory-artifacts` tips, confirming this is a genuine contention-sensitivity defect in `exec_subprocess`'s exit-code handling, not a one-off environmental fluke, and unrelated to cluster-2 (BC-1.18.006) content. Fix: harden `exec_subprocess`'s exit-code capture/propagation path to be correct under contention (e.g. avoid a race on the child's exit-status read). Not fixed this burst — out of state-manager's scope (crate source); routed to the E-12 Engine Governance follow-up story / next hook-hardening pass, alongside the D-1186 `pr-manager-completion-guard` item above. |

| **[D-1172] Carry-forward advisory — BC-1.18.005 `read_changelog_item_count` closing-fence heuristic undercount risk, anchored BC-1.18.009/cluster-4** | **OPEN 2026-09-06 — does NOT block; anchored to BC-1.18.009's own spec-evolution burst (cluster 4)** | Surfaced by the adversary at O2/pass-10 and re-flagged ADVISORY at pass-12: BC-1.18.005's `read_changelog_item_count` closing-fence heuristic (`after_open.find("\n---")`) can undercount changelog items if the frontmatter body itself contains a `---` line. **HARMLESS in cluster-1**: the item-count trigger is `tracing::warn!` + `Continue` (non-blocking) — an undercount only delays a fire, the safe direction, never a false block. **Becomes LOAD-BEARING in BC-1.18.009 (cluster 4, B1 rotation)** once that cluster's roll/block outcome depends on an accurate count against unbounded growth.
